Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Chapter 7 
Recommendations 

its outstanding concerns regarding the outage coordination methodology.222  In addition, the 
MMU submitted initiatives in the SPP Roadmap process related to outage policy. 223      

SPP made some enhancements per the MMU recommendation and closed the initiative.  SPP’s 
closure of the initiatives submitted by the MMU shows that they believe this issue is addressed. 

The MMU disagrees that its recommendations have been fully addressed and continues to 

recommend SPP make needed improvements to its outage coordination methodology, 

particularly in light of the central role outages play in the proposed accreditation 

methodologies.  With the recommendation remaining incomplete, the MMU is taking the 

opportunity to re-emphasis the importance of this recommendation and broaden its scope.  In 

particular, the MMU recommends focusing on how the outage study and approval process 

interacts with all aspects of reliability, transmission hedging, and the market, including energy 

adequacy.   

In the last few years, the region has experienced several instances where, during mild weather in 

shoulder seasons with average load, generation has been tight, partly due to approved, planned, 

and maintenance outages.  Additionally, the MMU has observed instances of denied outages 

taken as forced outages, outaged accredited capacity during energy emergencies, outage 

extensions lasting many times longer than the original approved outage, misuse of perpetual 

outages to get around the tariff retirement or replacement requirements, and scope of work 

changes within the same outage without evaluation, study, or approval.   

The proposed accreditation methodologies, currently under review at FERC, rely on accurate 

accounting and categorization of outages.  At the same time, the outage coordination 

methodology sits wholly outside the tariff and lacks sufficient detail.  For example, SPP’s outage 

coordination methodology does not clearly describe how it studies or approves outages .  It also 

allows for outages to be scheduled and approved by SPP without sufficient information included 

in the outage submission for SPP to discern whether the outage is legitimate or conforms with 

scheduling standards.  As such, the MMU believes the current outage coordination 

methodology is not sufficient to ensure energy adequacy or reliability and needs to be, at least 

in part, in the tariff to ensure consistent application and enforceability.  

The MMU recommends that SPP revise its tariff to, at a minimum, sufficiently describe the 

outage scheduling process, clearly delineate roles and responsibilities of SPP and its members in 

222 2022 Annual State of the Market report, Chapter 7 Recommendations, 2019.3 Update and Improve 
Outage Coordination Methodology 
223 SIR315 - WWE MMU R7 

State of the Market 2023 

241 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Chapter 7 
Recommendations 

the process, describe the associated timelines and requirements in the process, and define 

classifications of outages.    

2018.1  LIMIT THE EXERCISE OF MARKET POWER  BY CREATING A 

BACKSTOP FOR PARAMETER CHANGES 

In its 2018 SPP annual state of the market report, the MMU recommended that SPP strengthen 

the language regarding non-dollar-based parameters so that the expectation for the basis of 

these values is clear and well-defined and based on the actual capabilities of the resource.  This 
recommendation was added as an initiative 224 on the SPP Roadmap in 2020 as a high priority.  

As of the timing of this report, work has not started on this initiative.   

This MMU is highlighting the need for this recommendation to be addressed due to continued 

concerns regarding non-dollar-based parameters being used to manipulate market results and 

prices.  As noted in the 2018 recommendation, the market is vulnerable to the exercise of 

market power by the manipulation of resources’ non-dollar-based parameters and also that 

non-dollar-based parameters should not be manipulated to affect market clearing.   

The MMU continues to recommend tariff changes to be made to address the potential to 

impact market results and prices through the manipulation of non-dollar-based resource 

parameters.  The MMU recommends SPP include language in its tariff that requires information 

such as registration data, resource offers, and outage information to reasonably reflect the 

actual capabilities of the resources in the market. 

7.3  WINTER WEATHER REPORT – CRITICAL RECOMMENDATIONS 

As part of its review and analysis of the February 2021 and December 2022 winter weather 

events, the MMU published reports outlining multiple recommendations for consideration by 

SPP and stakeholders in the areas of resources, price formation, scheduling and dispatch, and 

gas-electric coordination.  The MMU has presented its findings to SPP, stakeholders, and FERC 

in multiple forums.  The recommendations below represent three critical areas the MMU 

continues to emphasize in those discussions. 

WWE1  ENSURE AVAILABILITY OF RESOURCES  

An accurate measure of resource-level availability on a seasonal basis to meet seasonal 

demands remains on the MMU’s list of critical recommendations for reliability.  An inadequate 

224 SIR 22 - Limit Market Power Through Physical Parameters 

State of the Market 2023 

242 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Chapter 7 
Recommendations 

estimate of capacity that will be available during peak demand or a system shock such as a 

weather event leads to the reliance on neighboring regions and unaccredited capacity to keep 

the lights on.  If the scope of the system shock is large enough, as it was in February 2021, it 

leads to load shed.   

In July 2022, the SPP Board of Directors approved the policy paper, recommended by SPP and 

approved by the Market and Operations Policy Committee (MOPC) and the Supply Adequacy 

Working Group (SAWG), for performance based accreditation (PBA) for conventional resources 

using a variant of a demand-weighted probabilistic equivalent forced outage rate demand 
(EFORd).225  SPP filed this methodology, along with a modified effective load carrying capability 
(ELCC) methodology for variable energy resources, with FERC in late February 2024.226  The 
MMU subsequently filed its comments in late March.227  Using performance based accreditation 

and effective load carrying capability for resource accreditation is an improvement over the 

status quo and is a first step in improving resource accreditation.  However, follow-up items 

remain.  For instance, one follow-up item relates to the weighting of PBA accredited resources 

during the highest value hours.  Another follow-up item is in regard to the treatment of out of 

management control outages for variable energy resources using effective load carrying 

capability.   

In order to improve resource adequacy, and to ensure non-discriminatory treatment of 

resources, the MMU recommends that SPP and its stakeholders continue to work on these 

important and necessary follow-up initiatives.  

WWE2  ESTABLISH INCENTIVE MECHANISM FOR  ACCREDITED 

CAPACITY 

The MMU continues to see the need for implementing both market and non-market 

mechanisms to incentivize resource attributes that enhance reliability through resiliency and 

availability.  The MMU recommends the development of market and non-market incentives for 

“reliability attributes” in conjunction with the development of an accurate measurement for 

resource availability as two legs of a three-legged stool that ensures reliability through 

measurements, incentives, and requirements.  The MMU proposed a set of market mechanisms 

to incentivize existing resources to increase their availability to the market, and efficiently 

schedule maintenance outages, through valuing the availability of capacity when it is needed in 

225 SPP Board of Directors action: 
https://www.spp.org/documents/67635/bod_mc%20minutes%202022%2007%2026.pdf 
226 FERC Docket ER24-1317 
227 MMU Comments on ER24-1317.pdf 

State of the Market 2023 

243 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Chapter 7 
Recommendations 

the region.  By placing a value of availability, participants can make economic choices on which 

resource attributes can be improved to most economically increase availability based on 

resource type, geographic location, and environmental and government policies.   

The MMU initiative was ranked as “high” in the 2023 SPP Roadmap prioritization.  However, little 

progress has been made and stakeholders have expressed a desire to wait for results from the 
proposed Performance Credit Mechanism pursued in the ERCOT market.228   

The MMU included recommendations in its winter weather report to allow for meaningful 

incentives for availability noting that, to the extent that a resource is more available there should 

be incentives, and to the extent that a resource is less available, there should be disincentives.  

The MMU continues to recommend the establishment of an incentive mechanism for actual 

resource performance for accredited capacity.  As mentioned above, despite improvements 

made by the proposed Performance Based Accreditation methodology for conventional 

resources and ELCC methodology for variable energy resources, the MMU does not anticipate 

this new accreditation approach alone to sufficiently incentivize resources to be more available.  

WWE3  ESTABLISH MORE FREQUENT RESOURCE ADEQUACY 

REQUIREMENT 

The MMU continues to emphasize the uniqueness of seasonal demands and the resource 

attributes needed to meet those demands.  The MMU recommends that SPP resource adequacy 

requirements address resource needs to meet the demand in each season independently.  While 

the region tends to experience the greatest demand and system shocks during summer and 

winter, availability tends to be lowest in the spring and fall seasons, which have no obligation 

nor requirement.   

SPP is currently working with stakeholders to change the winter season resource obligation into 

a requirement, complete with penalty charges for deficiency.  SPP and stakeholders filed an 

initial tariff revision establishing a winter resource adequacy requirement in September 2023.  

However, the filing was rejected by FERC on the grounds that there was not a reasonable 

expectation of availability for those resources accredited to meet the requirement.  A new filing 

is expected in the coming months.  

The MMU is engaged in the stakeholder process in support of adding a winter requirement, but 

additionally recommends the same effort be undertaken for the spring and fall seasons.  

228 The Performance Credit Mechanism has elements similar to the MMU’s proposed availability incentive 
approach. 

State of the Market 2023 

244 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Chapter 7 
Recommendations 

Moreover, to ensure resources are still able to complete necessary maintenance, the MMU 

recommends coupling the outage coordination process with the supply adequacy process to 

ensure regional availability needs and individual maintenance needs can be balanced without 

impacting reliability.  The MMU sees the seasonal resource adequacy requirement as the final 

leg of a balanced approach which uses accurate measurements, meaningful incentives, and 

adequate requirements to ensure reliability.    

7.4  PREVIOUS RECOMMENDATIONS 

The MMU has provided recommendations to improve market design in each of our previous 

annual state of the market reports since the launch of the Integrated Marketplace in 2014.  

While SPP and its stakeholders have found ways to effectively address some of the MMU’s 

concerns, there remain many outstanding recommendations.  A high-level summary of each of 

these recommendations, including recent updates and current status are outlined below.  

Additional information for these recommendations can be found in the corresponding annual 

state of the market report. 

2022.1  CONSIDER LIMITATIONS ON VIRTUAL TRADING DURING 

EMERGENCY CONDITIONS 

In 2022, the MMU published a paper examining the impact of virtual trading during the 
February 2021 winter weather event.229  Based on the results of the analysis performed, the 
MMU submitted an initiative230 on the SPP Roadmap seeking to study the effectiveness of virtual 

transactions during the winter weather event and identify any potential lessons learned or 

recommendations going forward.  This initiative was prioritized as low, placed on the parking lot 

of initiatives, and withdrawn.  The MMU recommends that SPP and stakeholders consider the 

recommendations made in the MMU report on virtual bidding behavior and, at a minimum, 

monitor metrics on the efficacy of virtual transactions as part of routine market analysis. 

2022.3  IMPROVE SITUATIONAL AWARENESS OF TRANSMISSION 
UPGRADES AND IMPROVE PROCESS TO REASSIGN 
PROJECTS 

Delays in transmission upgrades can significantly contribute to congestion.  This can have 

significant ramifications for market outcomes, and costs paid by ratepayers.  Moreover, resource 

229 Virtual activity during the 2021 winter weather event: An analysis 
230 SIR 325 - WWE MMU SD5 

State of the Market 2023 

245 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Chapter 7 
Recommendations 

decisions, including generator interconnection, are made based on expectations and 

information on the status of transmission upgrades.  

In the 2022 Annual Report, the MMU noted a significant number of transmission projects and 
upgrades that were behind expected relevant deadlines.231  Some of those projects were likely 

still in process several years beyond their expected in-service dates.  In many cases, public 

reporting of those projects’ statuses provided three separate “in-service” dates, none of which 

provided clear measures of the actual expected in-service date and delays. 

Given the lack of transparency, the MMU recommended that an “Original Project Owner 

Indicated In-Service Date” be added to the public reports.  This will allow full transparency into 

delays within the projects.  It is the understanding of the MMU that not all designated 

transmission owners respond with an Indicated In-Service Date.  The MMU recommended that 

this date be required and that the supplied date be benchmarked.  Furthermore, the MMU 

recommended that there should be regular updates from the transmission developer indicating 

the status of the project, along with detailed descriptions of the delaying issues.  This detail 

should be at a granular enough level to be able to discern the entity and upgrades delaying the 

project.  This additional information can improve situational awareness, and aid resource 

developers and decision makers. 

Throughout 2023, SPP and their stakeholders began implementing revisions and providing 

guidance for tracking meaningful in-service dates and delays from the base-level stakeholder 
groups all the way to SPP’s Board.232  SPP staff also initiated quarterly reports which identified 

delays by company and estimated length of the delays.  These actions represent marked 

improvements in tracking, transparency, and accountability. 

In the 2022 Annual Report, the MMU also recommended that SPP improve tariff clarity to assist 

in determining if transmission projects should be reassigned.  The SPP tariff affords the RTO to 
ensure that “due diligence” has been taken to develop projects in a timely manner.233  The MMU 

recommend that the SPP and stakeholders develop a clear set of guidelines as to what 

constitutes due diligence, including a potential for deciding on when to move a project to 

another developer.  To date, this remains an outstanding set of recommendations for SPP and 

stakeholders.

2022.4  APPROVE CONGESTION HEDGING MECHANISMS TO 

ENHANCE EQUITY 

In July 2019, the Holistic Integrated Tariff Team (HITT) published its recommendation report, 
including 21 board-approved recommendations.234  Marketplace enhancement recommendation 

one (HITT M1), “Implement congestion hedging improvements” remains outstanding.  After 

years of discussion in the Market Working Group and a failure to come to consensus, the Board 

of Directors intervened, directing SPP, with input from stakeholders, to develop a proposal.  In 

January 2023, SPP staff presented an updated HITT M1 recommendation to the Markets and 

Operations Policy Committee.  While the new proposal does not address the inherent mismatch 

between contract paths and the economic delivery of energy, it incorporates a much broader 

suite of recommendations than the original.   

The recommendation package comprises nine components, which target the upstream, mid-

stream, and down-stream aspects of the congestion hedging process.  The upstream 

components focus on model alignment between transmission service studies and the 

congestion hedging process.  The mid-stream components center around the mechanics within 

the long-term congestion right and auction revenue right allocations.  The downstream 

recommendations focus on the distribution of excess auction revenues and stakeholder 

education.  The package also incorporates a feedback loop from the congestion hedging 

process to the planning process. 

Revision request 591-HITT M1 congestion hedging improvements passed the SPP board of 

directors in February 2024 and is currently awaiting FERC review.  While the revision request only 

addresses roughly half of the available congestion hedges in SPP, the MMU believes it is a step 

in the right direction to obtaining better equity among members’ abilities to hedge congestion 

risks. 

2021.1  EXPAND MULTI-CONFIGURATION COMBINED CYCLE 

RESOURCE MODEL TO INCLUDE ADDITIONAL RESOURCE 
TYPES 

In the 2021 report,235 the MMU recommended SPP expand the multi-configuration combined 

cycle resource model or create a new multi-configuration model to include additional resource 

types that have multiple operating modes, or configurations.  This recommendation comes in

response to observed inefficiencies from participants attempting to optimize their plant’s 

schedule without the benefit of such logic.  The MMU noted that SPP’s multi-configuration 

combined cycle resource model provides the market several efficiency gains by optimizing the 

schedule of configurations, improving participant abilities to offer different parameters for each 

configuration, and providing real-time operational awareness. 

Additional information, including further potential applications of the multi-configuration 

combined cycle resource model, is documented in the 2021 report.   

2020.2  ENHANCE MARKET-TO-MARKET EFFICIENCIES THROUGH 

COLLABORATION WITH MISO 

In 2019 and 2020, the MMU worked with the MISO market monitor on a series of 

recommendations for the Joint Regional State Committee / Organization of MISO States Seams 

Liaison Committee.  Based on the results of the joint study, the MMU recommended in the 2020 

annual report to evaluate the processes and mechanisms used to effectuate the market-to-
market agreement between SPP and MISO.236  Several items identified by the market monitors 

have been addressed by SPP and MISO including activation of MISO market-to-market 

constraints in the SPP day-ahead market and increasing available relief of MISO resources.  The 

MMU continues to recommend that SPP and its stakeholders address inefficiencies in the 

market-to-market agreement between SPP and MISO.  Remaining inefficiencies are the 

automation of coordination tests and addressing power swings.  These inefficiencies are listed in 
the market-to-market improvements initiative237 and are currently on the SPP Roadmap as a 

medium priority.   

2020.3  RAISE OFFER FLOOR TO -$100/MWH 

In the 2020 report, the MMU recommended raising the energy offer floor to -$100/MWh.  This 

recommendation was the result of analysis performed by the MMU which observed resources 

offering at the current offer floor of -$500/MWh and setting price.  These offers did not 

represent cost, were often costly to the offering resource, and were harmful to nearby resources.  

As noted in the 2020 report, the MMU believes that raising the offer floor is a simple and cost-

effective solution that avoids any limitation of what costs can be included in a market offer.  This 
initiative238 is on the SPP Roadmap and is currently ranked as a low priority.   

236 2020 SPP Annual State of the Market Report, Chapter 8, 2020.2 Enhance market-to-market efficiencies 
through collaboration with MISO

2019.1  IMPROVE PRICE FORMATION  

In the 2019 report, the MMU identified circumstances where market prices provided neither 

short-term nor a long-term economic incentive to ensure reliability and recommended SPP and 

stakeholders review price formation during scarcity events and establish graduated demand 

curves that incentivize proper price formation.   

In the MMU report on the February 2021 winter weather event, the MMU made 

recommendations related to improving price formation during emergency and scarcity 

conditions.  In that report, the MMU highlighted situations where price signals did not 

accurately reflect underlying conditions.  These recommendations, which were aimed at 

improving pricing outcomes, closely align with the 2019 recommendation to improve price 

formation.   

SPP is currently developing an approach to address pricing issues during load shedding events 

including a methodology to determine a value of lost load (VOLL)  While the MMU supports this 

effort, the MMU continues to recommend that enhancements that improve price formation 

during emergencies and near emergencies should also be made.  The importance of this is 

heightened given significant potential changes to MISO’s operating reserve demand curve and 

value of loss load approaches.  Should MISO enact these proposed changes, they may have the 

potential to significantly affect SPP’s ability to attract imports and retain exports. 

2019.2  INCENTIVIZE CAPACITY PERFORMANCE 

In the 2019 report, the MMU recommended that SPP and its stakeholders work to incentivize 

capacity performance and suggested potential options.  In 2021, as part of its February 2021 

winter weather event report, the MMU also made recommendations regarding capacity 

adequacy and performance, many of which align with this recommendation.  As such, the MMU 

believes this recommendation will be addressed through those stakeholder processes focused 

on enhancements needed as a result of the February 2021 winter weather event. 

2018.2  ENHANCE CREDIT RULES TO ACCOUNT FOR KNOWN 

INFORMATION IN ASSESSMENTS 

In the 2018 report, the MMU made recommendations to improve SPP’s credit policy in light of a 

credit default in the PJM market that resulted in significant financial impacts to its market 

participants.  The MMU engaged with SPP’s Credit Practice Working Group regarding this issue 

and the potential impact to SPP and its market participants.  SPP stakeholders proposed a two-

phase approach to mitigate SPP’s exposure.   

State of the Market 2023 

249 

 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Chapter 7 
Recommendations 

The Credit Practices Working Group has developed and implemented the first phase of credit 

policy enhancements.  These enhancements, which included the development and 

implementation of a volumetric minimum collateral requirement, were a material improvement.  

The second phase focused on incorporating forward-looking known information into the 

financial security calculations.  SPP staff reviewed options for a forward-looking approach in 

2021, including a mark-to-auction process.  While no phase two changes were implemented, the 

MMU continues to recommend that SPP and stakeholders evaluate ways to incorporate 

forward-looking information in the credit evaluation process.  

In July 2022, FERC issued an order to show cause to four ISO/RTO’s as to why their tariffs were 

just and reasonable in absence of either of (1) mark-to-auction mechanisms for the calculation 

of financial transmission right (FTR) market participants’ collateral requirements and/or (2) 

volumetric minimum collateral requirements for FTR market participants.  SPP’s phase one 

efforts included the development and implementation of a volumetric minimum collateral 

requirement.  However, SPP did not elect to develop and implement mark-to-auction 

mechanisms.  SPP therefore was directed to show cause, as to why its tariff is just and 

reasonable without mark-to-auction mechanisms.  SPP filed comments in support of its current 

tariff whereas the MMU filed comments in support of mark-to-auction.  FERC has yet to provide 

a final rule.    FERC ruled that SPP did not provide adequate support for not having mark-to-

auction collateral pricing and in September 2023, FERC issued a 206 proceeding giving SPP 60 

days to either show cause as to why its tariff remains just and reasonable and not unduly 

discriminatory or preferential.  SPP responded to FERC in November, requesting an abeyance on 

the 206 proceedings until June 2024.  In this response, SPP stated they would bring back a 

mark-to-auction approach which works within SPP’s current credit and transmission congestion 

rights processes.   

The MMU filed in support of the abeyance request and FERC accepted.  This new mark-to-
auction approach239 is currently going through the early stages of the stakeholder process and 

SPP has requested an extension on the abeyance until August 2024.  The MMU is in support of 

the proposed mark-to-auction approach and believes it reduces the risks of under 

collateralization.  There are protests against the current proposed approach from some 

members surrounding the speed that the approach is taking through the stakeholder process, 

the burdens from the increases to collateral requirements, and the need for a balance of 

planning period transmission congestion rights process. 

239 RR-615 TCR mark-to-auction credit requirement

2018.5  IMPROVE REGULATION MILEAGE PRICE FORMATION   

In the 2018 annual state of the market report, the MMU recommended SPP staff review the 

performance of regulation mileage and develop potential approaches to improve regulation 

mileage price formation.  In addition, based on independent analysis, the MMU recommended 

SPP staff address two inefficiencies; one in the calculation of the factor used to determine 

average expected mileage, and one in the calculation of the clearing price for mileage.   

SPP drafted revision request 504 – improved economic incentive of regulation mileage in August 
2022 to address both MMU recommendations.240  Though it passed the SPP stakeholder process 

in December 2022, it was identified that additional edits were needed to ensure the governing 

language is consistent with the intent of the revision request.  This clean-up revision request 

(RR624 Improved Economic Incentive of Regulation Mileage Clean-Up) is currently in the late 
stages of the stakeholder process.241  SPP currently anticipates implementation in October 2024. 

Once implemented, the MMU expects these changes to adequately address the 

recommendations regarding regulation mileage. 

2017.2  ENHANCE COMMITMENT OF RESOURCES TO INCREASE 

RAMPING FLEXIBILITY 

In 2017, the MMU recommended that SPP and its stakeholders address the issue of inadequate 

ramping flexibility by modifying its market rules to enhance the commitment of resources and 

increase ramping flexibility.  The MMU noted potential options to address the commitment 

concerns and recommended SPP and its stakeholders explore options, such as those noted, to 
enhance commitment of resources and increase flexibility.  This initiative242 was on the SPP 

Roadmap and was ranked as a high priority.  However, it was closed in 2023, as the RTO 

combined it under SIR7 (SIR7 Decommitment and SIR9 Enhanced Commitment), which has a 

medium priority.   

2017.3  ENHANCE MARKET RULES FOR ENERGY STORAGE 

RESOURCES 

SPP implemented its design for energy storage for compliance with FERC Order No. 841 in 2021.  

While the MMU filed supportive comments for the implementation of energy storage in the SPP

market, the MMU noted further areas of enhancements to be considered with electric storage 

integration.   

Multiple initiatives were added to the SPP Roadmap to address the additional enhancements 

needed to fully integrate electric storage resources in the SPP markets.  The MMU previously 

made comments regarding an SPP initiative that would make enhancements to the energy 

storage design.  However, the MMU recommendation for inclusion of mitigation measures for 

excessively low offers was not reflected in the initiatives that were added to the SPP Roadmap.  

The MMU continues to recommend that SPP and stakeholders consider the implementation of 

mitigation measures for excessively low offers in the market. 

2017.4  ADDRESS INEFFICIENCY CAUSED BY SELF -COMMITTED 

RESOURCES 

The MMU recommended in the 2017 report that SPP and its stakeholders address the 

inefficiencies caused when market participants self-commit their resources in the market.  The 

MMU then conducted an in-depth study of self-commitment practices and associated 
inefficiencies in 2019.243  

The MMU continues to recommend that SPP and its stakeholders explore and develop ways to 

reduce instances of self-commitment of resources outside of the market solution, including 
considering adding an additional day to the optimization process.  In October 2021, initiatives244 

were added to the SPP Roadmap to implement certain enhancements that would address some 
of these issues.  SPP submitted revision request 612 (multi-day economic commitment245) in 

January 2024.  This revision request expands the unit commitment horizon for economic 

commitments, which would allow long lead time resources more opportunity to be considered 

as part of an economic commitment study rather than self-committing in the market.  The MMU 

submitted comments on the revision request regarding enhancements that are needed to 

address the potential for market manipulation.   

2017.5  ADDRESS INEFFICIENCY WHEN FORECASTED RESOURCES 

ARE UNDER-SCHEDULED DAY-AHEAD 

The MMU noted in its 2017 report that the systematic under-scheduling of wind resources in the 

day-ahead market can contribute to distorted price signals, suppressing real-time prices and

affecting revenue adequacy for all resources.246  This also poses a problem for resource 

adequacy as the current low prices in the SPP market do not support new entry of any resource 

type except wind (see Section 4.4).  With the understanding that variable energy resources are 

generally able to produce close to a forecasted amount, the MMU recommended that this issue 

be addressed through market incentives and rule changes.  These changes would focus on 

market inefficiencies associated with on the operation of variable energy resources, forecasted 

supply, and barriers to participate for virtual transactions.  The MMU has included this 

recommendation in each annual state of the market report since 2017 and included the 

recommendation in a special section of the 2022 annual report reemphasizing 
recommendations from previous reports that have not been addressed247.  Additional details 

regarding the recommendation can be found in those corresponding reports. 

The MMU raised this issue to the SPP Board of Directors in April 2023, noting its persistent 

impact on market efficiency.  The Board directed stakeholders to develop a timeline to work on 

this initiative.  SPP began working on this initiative, in collaboration with the MMU in early 2024.   

2014.4  ADDRESS ISSUES WITH THE DAY -AHEAD MUST OFFER 

REQUIREMENT 

The MMU continues to recommend that SPP and stakeholders eliminate the limited day-ahead 

must-offer provision and revise the physical withholding rules to include a penalty for non-
compliance or address the design weaknesses.  This initiative248 is on the SPP Roadmap and is 

currently ranked as a high priority.  

7.5  IMPLEMENTED RECOMMENDATIONS 

The following recommendations from previous reports were implemented in 2023. 

2018.3  DEVELOP COMPENSATION MECHANISM TO PAY FOR 

CAPACITY TO COVER UNCERTAINTIES 

In the 2018 report, the MMU recommended SPP develop a compensation mechanism to pay for 

capacity needed to address uncertainties in the market.

SPP market participants approved a revision request249 to implement an uncertainty product in

August 2022.  Implementation of the uncertainty product was completed July 6, 2023.250  The 

MMU considers this recommendation closed.  

2014.3  ADDRESS GAMING OPPORTUNITY FOR MULTI -DAY 

MINIMUM RUN TIME RESOURCES  

The MMU recommended changes to address a gaming opportunity in the market for resources 

with minimum run times greater than two days in its 2014 report.  While Tariff changes to 

address this concern were approved by the SPP board in 2018, subsequent changes were 

needed to address inconsistent tariff language that the revisions revealed but did not address.  
An associated revision request251 and an additional tariff modification was approved through the 
stakeholder process.  SPP filed these changes with FERC on May 7, 2020252 and the MMU filed 
comments in support of the tariff changes on June 12, 2020.253  These changes were 

implemented on October 1, 2023.  The MMU considers this recommendation closed.
